It is an executable file at the\napplication root. Invoked as `./ledger <command> [arguments]`, it hands the arguments to the\n`cli` package entry point `main(argv)` and exits with the integer that `main` returns.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `ledger` is a regular file at the application root with the executable bit set.\n- `ledger` calls `cli.main` with the arguments after the program name and exits with the returned\n  integer as its exit status.\n- Each command name in the Commands table of `sources/INSTRUCTIONS.md` is routed to a handler in\n  the handler table. Each handler is implemented by the story that owns that command.\n- The database is opened through `open_database` with the DSN selected by FEATURE-DATABASE-SELECTION.md.\n  Usage errors are detected before any database is opened.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC ledger-executable-present ===\nIntent: The application root contains an executable regular file named ledger.\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport os\n\nledger = Path(\"ledger\")\nassert ledger.is_file()\nassert os.access(ledger, os.X_OK)\n=== END AC ledger-executable-present ===\n\n=== AC ledger-entry-dispatch-in-process ===\nIntent: The cli entry point main returns an integer status for an unknown command name.\n\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(\"frobnicate\")\nassert code == 2\nassert out == \"\"\n=== END AC ledger-entry-dispatch-in-process ===\n\n=== AC ledger-entry-exit-status ===\nIntent: Invoking ./ledger with an unknown command exits with the status main returns.\nRequires: executable=python3; scope=test\n\nimport os\nimport subprocess\nimport tempfile\n\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as scratch:\n    env = {**os.environ, \"DATABASE_URL\": os.path.join(scratch, \"ledger.db\")}\n    result = subprocess.run(\n        [\"./ledger\", \"frobnicate\"],\n        capture_output=True, text=True, env=env,\n    )\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.returncode == 2\nassert result.stdout == \"\"\n=== END AC ledger-entry-exit-status ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- `ledger` contains no report, count, balance, or total computation. Every value comes from a query.\n- Application code never reads `sources/run.sh` or any seed file.\n- This story routes command names only. It does not implement the behavior of any command.\n- No database driver is imported here. Database access goes through `open_database` and the generated\n  persistence layer.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-CSV-OUTPUT.md ===\n# FEATURE: CSV Output\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261020 V1 |\n| Description | Successful commands render their result as CSV: one header row, then data rows, with no quoting and no trailing spaces. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-LEDGER-ENTRY.md, FEATURE-TEST-RUNNER.md |\n| Provides    | cli.output.render_csv(header, rows) |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nEvery successful command other than `init` writes CSV to standard output. This story supplies the\none renderer that all commands use, so the output conventions live in one place.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `render_csv(header, rows)` returns the text of one header row followed by one row per entry in\n  `rows`.\n- Fields are joined with `,`. No field is quoted. Each line ends with `\\n`, and the text ends with\n  `\\n`.\n- Values are written exactly as supplied. The renderer does not format numbers or trim text.\n- With no rows, the result is the header row alone.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC csv-rows-round-trip ===\nIntent: render_csv writes the header and every data row, unquoted, comma-separated, each line ending in a newline.\n\nfrom cli.output import render_csv\n\nheader = [\"account\", \"name\", \"transactions\", \"balance\"]\nrows = [[\"DRAWER1\", \"Front Drawer\", \"39\", \"2299.38\"], [\"PETTY\", \"Petty Cash\", \"21\", \"306.25\"]]\ntext = render_csv(header, rows)\nassert text.endswith(\"\\n\")\nraw = [line.split(\",\") for line in text.split(\"\\n\")[:-1]]\nassert raw == [header] + rows\n=== END AC csv-rows-round-trip ===\n\n=== AC csv-header-only-when-empty ===\nIntent: render_csv with no rows writes the header row alone.\n\nfrom cli.output import render_csv\n\nheader = [\"table\", \"rows\"]\ntext = render_csv(header, [])\nassert text.endswith(\"\\n\")\nraw = [line.split(\",\") for line in text.split(\"\\n\")[:-1]]\nassert raw == [header]\n=== END AC csv-header-only-when-empty ===\n\n=== AC csv-negative-amount-row ===\nIntent: render_csv writes a negative amount with its leading minus and no thousands separator.\n\nfrom cli.output import render_csv\n\nheader = [\"category\", \"net\"]\nrows = [[\"RENT\", \"-2700.00\"]]\ntext = render_csv(header, rows)\nraw = [line.split(\",\") for line in text.split(\"\\n\")[:-1]]\nassert raw == [header] + rows\n=== END AC csv-negative-amount-row ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Amounts reach this renderer already formatted as two-decimal text by FEATURE-AMOUNT-CENTS.md.\n  This renderer never converts a number to text.\n- Values containing a comma or newline are rejected by upstream validation before rendering, so no\n  quoting is ever required.\n- Diagnostics never pass through this renderer.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-EXIT-CODES.md ===\n# FEATURE: Exit Codes\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261020 V1 |\n| Description | Each command returns one of exit codes 0 to 4, and a failing command writes one diagnostic line to standard error and nothing to standard output. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-CSV-OUTPUT.md, FEATURE-AMOUNT-CENTS.md |\n| Provides    | cli.errors.LedgerError(code, message), cli.errors.run_command(handler) -> int |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nThe exit code is the contract a caller reads. This story gives every command one place to convert\nits outcome into an exit code and a diagnostic.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `LedgerError(code, message)` carries an exit code in `code` and a single-line message. Its code\n  is one of 1, 2, 3, or 4.\n- `run_command(handler)` calls `handler()`. The handler returns the CSV text to print.\n  - On success, `run_command` writes the returned text to standard output and returns `0`.\n  - On `LedgerError`, `run_command` writes one line to standard error and returns `error.code`,\n    writing nothing to standard output.\n- Exit codes follow the Exit codes table: 1 operational, 2 usage, 3 rejected, 4 not found.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC exit-success-writes-output-and-zero ===\nIntent: run_command returns 0 and writes exactly the handler's returned text to standard output on success.\n\nimport contextlib\nimport io\n\nfrom cli.errors import run_command\n\nbody = \"account,name\\nDRAWER1,Front Drawer\\n\"\nout = io.StringIO()\nerr = io.StringIO()\nwith contextlib.redirect_stdout(out), contextlib.redirect_stderr(err):\n    code = run_command(lambda: body)\nassert code == 0\nassert out.getvalue() == body\nassert err.getvalue() == \"\"\n=== END AC exit-success-writes-output-and-zero ===\n\n=== AC exit-failure-codes-mapped ===\nIntent: For each failure code 1 to 4, run_command returns that code, writes nothing to standard output, and writes exactly one line to standard error.\n\nimport contextlib\nimport io\n\nfrom cli.errors import LedgerError, run_command\n\ndef failing(code):\n    def handler():\n        raise LedgerError(code, \"rejected input\")\n    return handler\n\nfor code in [1, 2, 3, 4]:\n    out = io.StringIO()\n    err = io.StringIO()\n    with contextlib.redirect_stdout(out), contextlib.redirect_stderr(err):\n        result = run_command(failing(code))\n    assert result == code\n    assert out.getvalue() == \"\"\n    assert err.getvalue().count(\"\\n\") == 1\n=== END AC exit-failure-codes-mapped ===\n\n=== AC exit-error-carries-code ===\nIntent: LedgerError exposes the exit code it was constructed with.\n\nfrom cli.errors import LedgerError\n\nerror = LedgerError(3, \"rejected input\")\nassert error.code == 3\n=== END AC exit-error-carries-code ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Only the codes 0, 1, 2, 3, and 4 are produced by this mechanism.\n- A failing command never writes to standard output, including output it had begun to compute.\n- Standard error is not compared by acceptance. It carries one diagnostic line on failure.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-USAGE-ERRORS.md ===\n# FEATURE: Usage Errors\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261020 V1 |\n| Description | An unknown command, unknown load table, unknown report, or wrong argument count exits 2 before any database is opened. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-EXIT-CODES.md |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nUsage errors are the caller's mistake, not a data problem. They are reported with exit code 2 and\nare detected from the arguments alone.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- An unknown command name exits 2.\n- `load` with a table name other than `accounts`, `categories`, or `transactions` exits 2.\n- `report` with a name other than `balances`, `categories`, or `monthly` exits 2.\n- A command given a number of arguments outside the Commands table exits 2.\n- Running `ledger` with no arguments exits 2 and writes one usage line to standard error. This is\n  the conventional response chosen in DECISIONS.json (Q-003).\n- Each usage error writes nothing to standard output and one line to standard error.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC usage-unknown-command ===\nIntent: An unknown command exits 2, writes nothing to standard output, and writes one line to standard error.\n\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(\"frobnicate\")\nassert code == 2\nassert out == \"\"\nassert err.count(\"\\n\") == 1\n=== END AC usage-unknown-command ===\n\n=== AC usage-no-arguments ===\nIntent: Running with no arguments exits 2 with one usage line on standard error.\n\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner()\nassert code == 2\nassert out == \"\"\nassert err.count(\"\\n\") == 1\n=== END AC usage-no-arguments ===\n\n=== AC usage-unknown-load-table ===\nIntent: load with an unknown table name exits 2 without reading the file.\n\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(\"load\", \"nosuch\", \"unknown-table.csv\")\nassert code == 2\nassert out == \"\"\n=== END AC usage-unknown-load-table ===\n\n=== AC usage-unknown-report ===\nIntent: report with an unknown report name exits 2.\n\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(\"report\", \"nosuch\")\nassert code == 2\nassert out == \"\"\n=== END AC usage-unknown-report ===\n\n=== AC usage-extra-argument ===\nIntent: A command given more arguments than its Commands table entry exits 2.\n\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(\"count\", \"extra\")\nassert code == 2\nassert out == \"\"\n=== END AC usage-extra-argument ===\n\n=== AC usage-missing-argument ===\nIntent: A sub-verb given fewer arguments than its Commands table entry exits 2.\n\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(\"account\", \"get\")\nassert code == 2\nassert out == \"\"\n=== END AC usage-missing-argument ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Usage is checked before any database is opened. A usage error never touches the database.\n- `--help` is not treated as an unknown command by this story. Help output belongs to the help story.\n- Usage messages are diagnostics. They are never written to standard output.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-VALIDATE-CODE.md ===\n# FEATURE: Code Validation\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261020 V1 |\n| Description | Account and category codes are accepted only when they are 1 to 12 characters from A-Z and 0-9; any other code is rejected with exit 3. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-EXIT-CODES.md, FEATURE-TEST-RUNNER.md |\n| Provides    | cli.validate.validate_code(value) -> str |\n| Consumes    | cli.errors.LedgerError(code, message) |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nCodes are the keys of accounts and categories. Every command that accepts a code validates it here\nbefore the code reaches the database.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `validate_code(value)` returns `value` unchanged when it is 1 to 12 characters, each from `A-Z`\n  or `0-9`.\n- Any other value raises `LedgerError` with code 3.\n- Values are never normalized. Lowercase letters are rejected, not converted to uppercase.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC validate-code-accepts-boundaries ===\nIntent: validate_code returns the code unchanged for the shortest, longest, and all-digit valid codes.\n\nfrom cli.validate import validate_code\n\nshortest = \"A\"\nlongest = \"ABCDEFGHIJKL\"\ndigits = \"0042\"\nassert validate_code(shortest) == shortest\nassert validate_code(longest) == longest\nassert validate_code(digits) == digits\n=== END AC validate-code-accepts-boundaries ===\n\n=== AC validate-code-rejects-length ===\nIntent: validate_code rejects a code longer than 12 characters and an empty code with exit 3.\n\nfrom cli.errors import LedgerError\nfrom cli.validate import validate_code\n\ndef rejected_code(value):\n    try:\n        validate_code(value)\n    except LedgerError as error:\n        return error.code\n    return None\n\ntoo_long = \"ABCDEFGHIJKLM\"\nempty = \"\"\nassert rejected_code(too_long) == 3\nassert rejected_code(empty) == 3\n=== END AC validate-code-rejects-length ===\n\n=== AC validate-code-rejects-lowercase ===\nIntent: validate_code rejects lowercase letters with exit 3 and does not normalize them.\n\nfrom cli.errors import LedgerError\nfrom cli.validate import validate_code\n\ndef rejected_code(value):\n    try:\n        validate_code(value)\n    except LedgerError as error:\n        return error.code\n    return None\n\nlowercase = \"cash9\"\nassert rejected_code(lowercase) == 3\n=== END AC validate-code-rejects-lowercase ===\n\n=== AC validate-code-rejects-punctuation ===\nIntent: validate_code rejects hyphens, spaces, and trailing newlines with exit 3.\n\nfrom cli.errors import LedgerError\nfrom cli.validate import validate_code\n\ndef rejected_code(value):\n    try:\n        validate_code(value)\n    except LedgerError as error:\n        return error.code\n    return None\n\nhyphenated = \"CASH-9\"\nspaced = \"CASH 9\"\nnewline_ended = \"CASH9\\n\"\nassert Invoked as `./ledger <command> [arguments]`, it hands the arguments to the\n`cli` package entry point `main(argv)` and exits with the integer that `main` returns.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `ledger` is a regular file at the application root with the executable bit set.\n- `ledger` calls `cli.main` with the arguments after the program name and exits with the returned\n  integer as its exit status.\n- Each command name in the Commands table of `sources/INSTRUCTIONS.md` is routed to a handler in\n  the handler table. Each handler is implemented by the story that owns that command.\n- The database is opened through `open_database` with the DSN selected by FEATURE-DATABASE-SELECTION.md.\n  Usage errors are detected before any database is opened.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC ledger-executable-present ===\nIntent: The application root contains an executable regular file named ledger.\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport os\n\nledger = Path(\"ledger\")\nassert ledger.is_file()\nassert os.access(ledger, os.X_OK)\n=== END AC ledger-executable-present ===\n\n=== AC ledger-entry-dispatch-in-process ===\nIntent: The cli entry point main returns an integer status for an unknown command name.\n\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(\"frobnicate\")\nassert code == 2\nassert out == \"\"\n=== END AC ledger-entry-dispatch-in-process ===\n\n=== AC ledger-entry-exit-status ===\nIntent: Invoking ./ledger with an unknown command exits with the status main returns.\nRequires: executable=python3; scope=test\n\nimport os\nimport subprocess\nimport tempfile\n\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as scratch:\n    env = {**os.environ, \"DATABASE_URL\": os.path.join(scratch, \"ledger.db\")}\n    result = subprocess.run(\n        [\"./ledger\", \"frobnicate\"],\n        capture_output=True, text=True, env=env,\n    )\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.returncode == 2\nassert result.stdout == \"\"\n=== END AC ledger-entry-exit-status ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- `ledger` contains no report, count, balance, or total computation. It does not implement the behavior of any command.\n- No database driver is imported here. This story supplies the\none renderer that all commands use, so the output conventions live in one place.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `render_csv(header, rows)` returns the text of one header row followed by one row per entry in\n  `rows`.\n- Fields are joined with `,`. No field is quoted. Each line ends with `\\n`, and the text ends with\n  `\\n`.\n- Values are written exactly as supplied. The renderer does not format numbers or trim text.\n- With no rows, the result is the header row alone.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC csv-rows-round-trip ===\nIntent: render_csv writes the header and every data row, unquoted, comma-separated, each line ending in a newline.\n\nfrom cli.output import render_csv\n\nheader = [\"account\", \"name\", \"transactions\", \"balance\"]\nrows = line in text.split(\"\\n\")[:-1]]\nassert raw == [header] + rows\n=== END AC csv-rows-round-trip ===\n\n=== AC csv-header-only-when-empty ===\nIntent: render_csv with no rows writes the header row text.split(\"\\n\")[:-1]]\nassert raw == [header]\n=== END AC csv-header-only-when-empty ===\n\n=== AC csv-negative-amount-row ===\nIntent: render_csv writes a negative amount with its leading minus formatted as two-decimal text by FEATURE-AMOUNT-CENTS.md.\n  This renderer never converts a number to text.\n- Values containing a comma or newline are rejected by upstream validation before rendering, so no\n  quoting is ever required.\n- Diagnostics never pass through this renderer.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-EXIT-CODES.md ===\n# FEATURE: Exit Codes\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261020 V1 |\n| Description | Each command returns one of exit codes 0 to 4, and a failing command writes one diagnostic line to standard error and nothing to standard output. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-CSV-OUTPUT.md, FEATURE-AMOUNT-CENTS.md |\n| Provides    | cli.errors.LedgerError(code, message), cli.errors.run_command(handler) -> int |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nThe exit code is the contract a caller reads. This story gives every command one place to convert\nits outcome into an exit code and a diagnostic.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `LedgerError(code, message)` carries an exit code in `code` and a single-line message. Its code\n  is one of 1, 2, 3, or 4.\n- `run_command(handler)` calls `handler()`. The handler returns the CSV text to print.\n  - On success, `run_command` writes the returned text to standard output and returns `0`.\n  - On `LedgerError`, `run_command` writes one line to standard error and returns `error.code`,\n    writing nothing to standard output.\n- Exit codes follow the Exit codes table: 1 operational, 2 usage, 3 rejected, 4 not found.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC exit-success-writes-output-and-zero ===\nIntent: run_command returns 0 and writes exactly the handler's returned text to standard output on success.\n\nimport contextlib\nimport io\n\nfrom cli.errors import run_command\n\nbody = \"account,name\\nDRAWER1,Front Drawer\\n\"\nout = io.StringIO()\nerr = io.StringIO()\nwith contextlib.redirect_stdout(out), contextlib.redirect_stderr(err):\n    code = run_command(lambda: body)\nassert code == 0\nassert out.getvalue() == body\nassert err.getvalue() == \"\"\n=== END AC exit-success-writes-output-and-zero ===\n\n=== AC exit-failure-codes-mapped ===\nIntent: For each failure code 1 to 4, run_command returns that code, writes nothing to standard output, and writes exactly one line to standard error.\n\nimport contextlib\nimport io\n\nfrom cli.errors import LedgerError, run_command\n\ndef failing(code):\n    def handler():\n        raise LedgerError(code, \"rejected input\")\n    return handler\n\nfor code in [1, 2, 3, 4]:\n    out = io.StringIO()\n    err = io.StringIO()\n    with contextlib.redirect_stdout(out), contextlib.redirect_stderr(err):\n        result = run_command(failing(code))\n    assert result == code\n    assert out.getvalue() == \"\"\n    assert err.getvalue().count(\"\\n\") == 1\n=== END AC exit-failure-codes-mapped ===\n\n=== AC exit-error-carries-code ===\nIntent: LedgerError exposes the exit code it was constructed with.\n\nfrom cli.errors import LedgerError\n\nerror = LedgerError(3, \"rejected input\")\nassert error.code == 3\n=== END AC exit-error-carries-code ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Only the codes 0, 1, 2, 3, and 4 are produced by this mechanism.\n- A failing command never writes to standard output, including output it had begun to compute.\n- Standard error is not compared by acceptance. It carries one diagnostic line on failure.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-USAGE-ERRORS.md ===\n# FEATURE: Usage Errors\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261020 V1 |\n| Description | An unknown command, unknown load table, unknown report, or wrong argument count exits 2 before any database is opened. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-EXIT-CODES.md |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nUsage errors are the caller's mistake, not a data problem. Every command that accepts a code validates it here\nbefore the code reaches the database.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `validate_code(value)` returns `value` unchanged when it is 1 to 12 characters, each from `A-Z`\n  or `0-9`.\n- Any other value raises `LedgerError` with code 3.\n- Values are never normalized. Lowercase letters are rejected, not converted to uppercase.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC validate-code-accepts-boundaries ===\nIntent: validate_code returns the code unchanged for the shortest, longest, and all-digit valid codes.\n\nfrom cli.validate import validate_code\n\nshortest = \"A\"\nlongest = \"ABCDEFGHIJKL\"\ndigits = \"0042\"\nassert validate_code(shortest) == shortest\nassert validate_code(longest) == longest\nassert validate_code(digits) == digits\n=== END AC validate-code-accepts-boundaries ===\n\n=== AC validate-code-rejects-length ===\nIntent: 3\nassert rejected_code(empty) == 3\n=== END AC validate-code-rejects-length ===\n\n=== AC validate-code-rejects-lowercase ===\nIntent: validate_code rejects lowercase letters with exit 3 and does not
